A request sets `moreToCome` only for an unacknowledged write (`w: 0`), which gets no reply. `OP_COMPRESSED` carries snappy, zlib or zstd, chosen from `compressors` against the server's list, and the handshake and authentication commands are never compressed.
  - The handshake sends `hello` with `helloOk`, the client metadata and `saslSupportedMechs`. Authentication takes SCRAM-SHA-256 or SCRAM-SHA-1 through `std/crypto/sasl`, the strongest the server lists unless `authMechanism` names one, with `skipEmptyExchange`. The SCRAM-SHA-1 secret is the hex of `MD5("user:mongo:password")`; SCRAM-SHA-256 uses the password as given, and a non-ASCII password fails with `Unsupported` because the driver has no SASLprep. A refused login fails with `Authentication`, and `MONGODB-X509`, `tlsCertificateKeyFile` and `mongodb+srv://` fail with `Unsupported`.
  - Every server has a monitor green thread with its own connection, which polls `hello` every `heartbeat` milliseconds and can be woken to recheck sooner, but never more often than every 500. The topology is unknown, single, a replica set without or with a primary, or sharded; a primary is stale when its `setVersion` and `electionId` are behind the newest seen, members come from `hosts`, `passives` and `arbiters`, and a member whose `me` differs from the address used is dropped. A selection filters by read preference mode, tag sets and `maxStalenessSeconds`, keeps the servers within `local_threshold` of the fastest and fails with `Selection` after `server_selection_timeout`. `$readPreference` goes out for any mode but `primary` on a replica set or sharded topology, and on a single member that is neither a standalone nor a mongos, where `primary` is sent as `primaryPreferred`.
  - Each server has a pool of up to `max_pool_size` connections. An idle connection closes after `max_idle_time`, a caller waits up to `wait_queue_timeout` for one, and a network failure closes the idle connections of that server and starts a new generation.
  - Every command runs in a session, an implicit one drawn from a pool of server sessions (`lsid` as UUID binary subtype 4) unless the call names one, and `endSessions` goes out when the client closes. `$clusterTime` from every reply is carried on every request. A causal `Session` adds `afterClusterTime` from its `operationTime` to reads.
  - A read, and a write that carries a `txnNumber` on a server that supports it, is retried once on a freshly selected server after a network failure or a retryable error; `update_many` and `delete_many` are not retried. A server error becomes `Server` with its `errorLabels`, a `writeErrors` entry `Write` with the position in the batch, and a `writeConcernError` `WriteConcern`.
  - A transaction sends `startTransaction`, `autocommit: false` and the next `txnNumber` with its first command and the session's read concern with `afterClusterTime`, and runs on one primary. `commit_transaction` retries once and then asks for a majority write concern; `with_transaction` reruns its body for up to 120 seconds on `TransientTransactionError` and `UnknownTransactionCommitResult`. A transaction on a server without replica set support fails with `Unsupported`.
  - Not implemented: DNS seed lists, client certificates, SASLprep, streaming `hello` and exhaust cursors (the monitor polls, and `exhaustAllowed` is only encoded and decoded), tailable cursors, pipeline-style updates, aggregates over a database, sharded transaction pinning and `recoveryToken`, load-balanced mode, a minimum pool size, and typed documents through serde.
- `std/toml` parses into ordered tables and applies every rule of TOML 1.0.0, including the restrictions on redefining tables through headers, dotted keys, inline tables and arrays of tables. It agrees with Python's `tomllib` on the toml-test suite. Errors carry a line and column. `encode` writes plain values first and then `[table]` and `[[array]]` sections. `serialize` and `deserialize` bind types through serde, reading date-times as RFC 3339 text.
- `std/yaml` parses YAML 1.2.2 into ordered node trees and agrees with the yaml-test-suite on every case's event stream and JSON equivalent.
  - A scanner modelled on libyaml tokenizes the whole stream, tracking indentation, implicit keys, JSON-like adjacent values and the YAML 1.2 tab rules, with 32-byte vector scans over plain scalars and `simd.find_byte_str` for comment and line ends. Token text is a view into the source.
  - A recursive-descent parser turns tokens into `Event`s with resolved tags, and a composer builds `Node`s carrying line, column, tag, anchor and style.
  - Plain scalars resolve with the core schema. `!!str`, `!!int`, `!!float`, `!!bool`, `!!null` and `!!binary` are checked against their content; other tags stay on the node. Aliases share their anchored node. `Options.max_nodes` bounds the tree with every alias expanded, failing with `TooManyNodes`, and `max_depth` bounds nesting. Duplicate scalar keys are an error.
  - `encode` writes block style, quotes a scalar only when it would read back differently (including YAML 1.1 booleans, numbers and dates), writes multi-line text as literal blocks, and anchors any node reached more than once. `serialize` and `deserialize` bind types through serde, with `u8[]` as `!!binary`.
- `std/otel` keeps the provider in the green thread's context slot as an immutable `Context` of the active span, baggage, a suppression flag and the SDK.
  - `spawn` copies the slot, so a child green thread's spans parent to the spawning span. `install` and `setup_from_env` return a droppable `Sdk` that shuts down when released and put the `SdkHandle` into the calling green thread's slot, reaching the green threads it spawns afterwards. Programs install at the top of `main`. Every API call checks the slot first and returns a no-op value without allocating when no SDK is present.
  - Timestamps and durations come from `time.now_nanos` and `time.monotonic`. Span and trace ids come from a `std/rand` generator behind a lock. Samplers are always on, always off, trace-id ratio on the low 63 bits, and parent-based.
  - Batch processors for spans and logs queue to a bounded channel with a non-blocking send that counts drops. A worker green thread exports when a batch fills, when the delay passes, and on flush or shutdown. Periodic metric readers use the same control worker for flush and shutdown.
  - Metric instruments aggregate per attribute set, keyed by a canonical encoding of the sorted attributes, in one stream per reader, capped at 2000 series plus an `otel.metric.overflow` series. Readers collect cumulatively or as deltas, periodically or on demand.
  - Exporters encode OTLP once through a writer that emits protobuf through `std/protobuf` or the OTLP JSON mapping, and send with the context suppressed.
  - OTLP/HTTP posts, optionally gzipped, retrying 429, 502, 503 and 504 through `std/retry` with equal-jitter exponential backoff, where `Retry-After` raises the wait.
  - OTLP/gRPC makes unary `Export` calls on `opentelemetry.proto.collector.{trace,metrics,logs}.v1` through a `std/grpc` `Channel` dialled to the endpoint (h2c for `http://`, TLS for `https://`), gzipped under `grpc-encoding` when the exporter's `gzip` is set, with the exporter's headers as metadata and its timeout as the call deadline. The channel's own retry is off; the exporter's retry policy decides. CANCELLED, DEADLINE_EXCEEDED, ABORTED, OUT_OF_RANGE, UNAVAILABLE and DATA_LOSS are retried. RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED is retried only when `grpc-status-details-bin` carries a RetryInfo, whose delay replaces the backoff.
  - `setup_from_env` reads the standard `OTEL_*` variables. `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL=grpc` selects gRPC, defaulting to `http://localhost:4317`.
- `std/http` sits below `std/otel` in the import graph, so the hook runs through the context slot. `otel.Context` declares an `http.Ambient` as its first field, and `std/http` reads the slot as `*http.Ambient`, which relies on struct fields being laid out in declaration order. One constructor builds every `Context` and derives the ambient `Observer` from it: the observer is set exactly when an SDK is installed and the context is not suppressed, and `std/http` does nothing when it is absent. Processors and metric readers call exporters through `quietly`, so no exporter has to suppress its own telemetry.
  - `http.serve` and `serve_tls` open a server span per request over HTTP/1.1 and HTTP/2, with the parent extracted from `traceparent` and `client.address` from `Request.remote`, and record `http.server.request.duration`. `std/web` adopts that span, adds `http.route` and renames it to the method and route; it opens its own span only when its handler runs outside `http.serve`.
  - The client opens a CLIENT span named after the method for each attempt sent, redirects and retries included. Each span is under the caller's span, with `http.request.resend_count` from the second attempt. The client injects `traceparent`, `tracestate` and `baggage` into that attempt, sets `url.full` without userinfo, and ends the span when the body is delivered or the attempt fails, with `error.type` the status from 400 or the `http.Error` variant. It records `http.client.request.duration`.
  - `std/sql/driver` wraps every connection that `std/sql/postgres`, `std/sql/sqlserver`, `std/sql/mysql` and `std/sql/sqlite` return with `instrument_sql`, reading the `db.system.name` from the connection's `Dialect`. `std/orm` opens client spans with the operation and table; a nested database span merges into the outer one, so an ORM query produces one span.
- `std/protobuf` writes and reads the protobuf wire format without code generation. An `Encoder` appends into a buffer that doubles as needed. Signed integers sign-extend to ten bytes and `sint64` zigzag-encodes. `begin_message` returns a mark and `end_message` measures the body, shifts it right by the size of its length varint and writes the length, so nested messages need neither a second buffer nor padded lengths. Output matches `protoc --encode` byte for byte. A `Decoder` walks fields in order as number, wire type, integer value and a view of the payload. It rejects truncated or overlong varints, lengths past the end, field 0 and wire types 6 and 7 with `Malformed`, and groups with `Unsupported`. The `as_*` readers check the wire type and the `unpack_*` readers accept packed and single encodings.
  - `serialize[T]` and `deserialize[T]` take a struct whose fields carry their protobuf numbers as tags (see Structs) and run through `std/serde`: encoding builds a `serde.Tree` with `serde.to_tree` and writes it by the schema, decoding turns the wire bytes into a tree by the schema and reads it with `serde.read`. A tag is a comma-separated list of the field number, `sint`, `fixed`, `unpacked`, `required`, `key=sint`, `key=fixed`, `value=sint` and `value=fixed`; a field of an enum with payload variants is tagged `oneof`, each variant's `= n` index is the field number of its member, and a variant's payload tag has no number. Integers take the `int32`, `int64`, `uint32` or `uint64` encoding by width and sign, an enum of payload-free variants is a protobuf enum of the variants' indexes, a struct is an embedded message, a slice is repeated, packed unless tagged `unpacked`, and a map is a repeated entry message.
  - A singular field that holds its default is not written unless it declares a default of its own, an optional field is written whenever it holds a value, and a non-optional embedded message is always written. Decoding gives an absent field its declared default, `none` for an optional one and zero otherwise, takes the last value of a repeated singular field, merges repeated occurrences of an embedded message, collects repeated fields packed or not, and takes the last member of a `oneof`. It skips unknown fields, fields of the wrong wire type and enum values no variant has, checks strings for UTF-8, and fails with `Syntax` at a byte offset for malformed input, `TooDeep` for more than 100 levels of messages, `Mismatch` for a `T` that is not a struct and `Missing` for an absent `required` field. A pointer field such as `*Node?` carries a recursive message.
  - `std/protobuf/proto` reads `.proto` text with `parse(path, source)`. It accepts `syntax`, `package`, `import` with `public` and `weak`, `option`, `message`, nested messages and enums, `enum` with `allow_alias`, `oneof`, `map`, `repeated`, `optional`, proto2 `required` and `default`, `reserved`, `extensions`, `extend`, and `service` with `rpc` and `stream` on either side, and keeps options as written except that `default` and `json_name` are fields of `Field` and `packed` and `deprecated` change what the generator writes. It rejects a proto2 `group`, an `edition` and proto3 files that use `required`, `default`, extension ranges or a first enum value other than zero, bad or duplicate field numbers and names, reserved numbers and names, wrong map keys, empty oneofs, and nesting past 32 levels, each as one `proto.Error` variant with the file, line and column; `proto.message` formats it.
  - `read_files(paths, roots, read)` loads each path and its imports through `read`, which returns the text of a file or fails with `io.Error.NotFound`, tries the roots in order, rejects import cycles, lists the files with every file after the files it imports, gives each type its full name and replaces every type name with the full name of the message or enum it refers to. A name resolves by matching the whole written name against the innermost enclosing message and then each scope outwards through the package, then absolutely, and only through the file's own definitions, its imports and the `import public` closure of those. The loader touches no file system itself, so a program supplies `file.read_text` and a test supplies a map.
  - `generate(set, options)` writes one Low module per file, `a/b.proto` as `a/b/b.low`, with `Options.module` as the import path of the output directory. A message is a `pub struct` and an enum or oneof a `pub enum`, nested types named by joining the names of the messages around them (`Outer.Inner` is `OuterInner`, the oneof `choice` of `Scalars` is `ScalarsChoice`), with a number after a name that is already taken. Field names stay when they are `snake_case` and go through `std/text/naming` otherwise; a keyword gains a trailing `_`. An enum value loses the enum name as a prefix when a name remains. A singular message field is optional, a singular field or oneof member in a cycle of messages is `*T?`, a map is `[K: V]` with its key and value encodings in the tag, a proto2 field with a default keeps it as the field's default, a message without fields has one `bool` field `empty` on field number 536870911, and a type from another file is reached through an import of that file's module. A `service` with methods becomes, for service `Greeter`, a `GreeterClient` made by `greeter_client(channel)` with one function per method named after it in `snake_case` (a name another service of the file already took gains a number), whose call shape follows the `stream` keywords and whose types are the request and response messages; a `GreeterHandlers` struct with one function-valued field per method; and `register_greeter(server, handlers)`, which registers each method as `/package.Greeter/Method`. A service with no methods gets only the client. The output is a function of the files alone; extensions and other options are left out.
  - `low protogen -I dir,dir --module path -o outdir file.proto...` reads the files against the roots and writes the modules under `outdir`, imported files included. The generator is the library `compiler/protogen`, which `compiler/low/protogen.low` calls.
- `std/xml` reads XML 1.0 (fifth edition) with Namespaces from UTF-8, UTF-16 with a byte order mark, or any encoding `std/charset` knows by its declared label, converting to UTF-8 and normalising line endings first.
  - `xml.Reader` is a pull reader: `next` returns one token and `position` its line and column. Names arrive with namespaces resolved and are interned in a per-reader table. Text, CDATA, comments and attribute values are views into the input unless a reference or normalisation forces a copy, and 32-byte vector scans find the bytes that end them. The reader enforces well-formedness and the namespace constraints and agrees with libxml2 on what it accepts.
  - The internal subset is parsed. General entities expand, including markup, with recursion detection, a nesting limit of 40 and a total of 1 MiB plus eight times the input. Attribute-list declarations supply defaults and normalise tokenised types. External entities are never fetched and parameter entities are not expanded.
  - `xml.parse` builds an `Element` tree of owned strings with `attribute`, `child`, `children`, `text` and `find`. `xml.Writer` escapes text and attributes, splits CDATA around `]]>`, writes namespace declarations and indents element-only content. `serialize_value` and `deserialize` bind types through serde, mapping fields to attributes or child elements by local name, repeated elements to slices, and `xsi:nil` to `none`.
- Pipes block through channels. A writer flushing more than the pause threshold waits until the reader brings the unconsumed bytes below the resume threshold, or until the reader itself waits for more data, so a message larger than the thresholds cannot deadlock the pipe.
- `pool.open` stores `ConnectionHandle` views, so one connection serves a lease at a time and the pool, not the owner, closes it. Idle and lifetime limits are checked on acquire, release and `prune` against `clock.monotonic`, and a release hands the connection to the oldest waiter. A lease marks its connection broken on `Connect`, `Authentication`, `Closed`, `Timeout` or `Protocol` errors, and the pool closes it. A transaction left open at release is rolled back.
- `migrate.up` refuses any applied migration whose checksum differs (`Drift`), a pending version below the highest applied one (`OutOfOrder`) and duplicate or non-ascending versions. The lock is `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` on SQLite, `pg_advisory_lock` on PostgreSQL, `sp_getapplock` on SQL Server and `GET_LOCK` on MySQL and MariaDB, and each migration re-checks inside its transaction that it is not yet recorded. A migration that fails reports its own error, or the rollback's error when the rollback fails too; a lock that cannot be released fails with `Sql` unless a migration error is reported first. In a text migration a statement ends at a line ending in `;`, and `-- +begin` to `-- +end` keeps a block with several `;` as one statement.

#### Unicode

- `std/unicode` reads tables generated by `compiler/tablegen unicode` from UnicodeData.txt, CaseFolding.txt and DerivedNormalizationProps.txt of Unicode 16.0.0, the version Python's `unicodedata` reports, and agrees with it on every code point. `compiler/tablegen unicode` and `compiler/tablegen properties` also write `normalization_layout.low` and `property_layout.low`: constants for the record sizes, field offsets, trie shifts and masks, Hangul constants and packed property bit fields, and one `<table>_table()` accessor per `.bin` file that `embed`s it. The module sources read these instead of literals, and `layout_files_match_generator` in `compiler/tests/tablegen` fails when a committed layout file differs from the generator. `compiler/tablegen classes` writes the simple case folding orbits and the word set that `std/regex` embeds, as `### Regular expressions` describes.
- The tables are `embed`ded binary files. A two-level trie of 64-entry blocks covers code points below U+2FA40 and gives 0 above. It maps each code point to a 16-bit record id. Each record holds the canonical combining class and 16-bit offsets and lengths into a pool of `u32` code points for the full canonical decomposition, the full compatibility decomposition and the C and F case folding.
- Hangul syllables decompose and compose algorithmically. Decomposition is followed by a stable canonical reordering by combining class. Composition binary-searches a sorted table of primary composites, leaving out Full_Composition_Exclusion. ASCII input returns at once, or lowercased by `casefold` and `search_key`.
- Word, sentence and line break data live in their own tries, `word_*`, `sentence_*` and `line_*` `.bin` files of an index, 32-entry blocks and records, written by `compiler/tablegen segmentation` from WordBreakProperty.txt, SentenceBreakProperty.txt, LineBreak.txt, EastAsianWidth.txt, emoji-data.txt and UnicodeData.txt of Unicode 17.0.0, with `segmentation_layout.low` holding the shifts, masks, flag bits and class constants. A word record holds the Word_Break class and the Extended_Pictographic bit. A line record holds the Line_Break class after the LB1 resolution (AI, SG and XX to AL, SA to CM for Mn and Mc and to AL otherwise, CJ to NS), the East Asian Width F, W or H bit, the Extended_Pictographic and Cn bit, and the Pi and Pf bits of quotation marks. The tries cost 45,877 bytes (word), 47,343 bytes (sentence) and 43,546 bytes (line), each linked only when `words`, `sentences` or `line_breaks` is reachable.
- `word_end`, `sentence_end` and `grapheme_end` scan forward from a segment start with a small state record and the next code point, and look ahead only where a rule needs it (WB6, WB7b, WB12, SB8). WB4 and SB5 fold Extend, Format and ZWJ into the character before them. `line_breaks` scans the whole string once, because UAX #14 rules (LB15a, LB19a) read the character before a break position and a restart at an earlier break would change the answer. It implements LB1 to LB31 of Unicode 17.0.0 with no tailoring: LB9 and LB10 fold or replace CM and ZWJ, LB15a to LB15d, LB20a, LB28a with U+25CC and LB30b use the record bits, and the break after the last character is mandatory (LB3), as are the breaks after BK, LF, NL and a CR not followed by LF (LB4 and LB5). The default rules pass every line of WordBreakTest.txt, SentenceBreakTest.txt and LineBreakTest.txt of 17.0.0.
- `search_key(s)` equals Python's `''.join(c for c in unicodedata.normalize('NFKD', s.casefold()) if not unicodedata.combining(c))`. It skips reordering, since every reordered character is dropped.

#### Hostnames

- `std/net/punycode` implements RFC 3492 with 32-bit overflow checks. Its encoder and decoder agree with every sample of section 7.1 and with Python's `punycode` codec on 300 random strings.
- `std/net/idna` follows UTS #46 revision 33 for Unicode 16.0.0: map, normalise to NFC through `std/unicode`, break at the label separators, decode `xn--` labels, then validate each label against the criteria `V1` to `V7` and `U1`, with the RFC 5893 Bidi rule when `check_bidi` is set and the RFC 5892 ContextJ rule for U+200C and U+200D when `check_joiners` is set. `to_ascii` encodes the labels with Punycode and applies the length limits when `verify_dns_length` is set. `use_std3_ascii_rules` restricts ASCII code points to letters, digits and `-`; the mapping table of this version has no `disallowed_STD3` entries. ContextO and `IgnoreInvalidPunycode` are not provided.
- `compiler/tablegen idna <ucd directory> <output directory>` generates the tables from IdnaMappingTable.txt of `Public/idna/16.0.0`, and DerivedBidiClass.txt, DerivedJoiningType.txt and UnicodeData.txt of Unicode 16.0.0. It writes `idna_mapping.bin` (sorted ranges with a status and a pool offset, adjacent equal ranges merged), `idna_pool.bin` (the mapped UTF-8 text, shared), `idna_classes.bin` (sorted ranges holding the Bidi class, the joining type and the mark flag) and `idna_layout.low`. Lookups binary-search the ranges.
- The conformance fixture runs every line of IdnaTestV2.txt for 16.0.0 through `to_unicode` and through `to_ascii` with nontransitional and transitional processing: 6,387 of 6,387 lines agree on the text and on the failed check, and the two lines whose source holds an unpaired surrogate are not run because a `str` cannot hold one.
- `std/net/publicsuffix` stores the rules as a trie of reversed labels in UTF-8, written by `compiler/tablegen publicsuffix <public_suffix_list.dat> <output directory>` into `suffix_nodes.bin`, `suffix_labels.bin`, `suffix_version.bin` and `suffix_layout.low`. Each node records whether a rule ends there, whether a wildcard rule `*.` hangs from it, whether an exception rule `!` ends there, and for each whether the rule is in the private section. The longest rule wins, an exception rule wins over any other and drops its leftmost label, and a name no rule matches takes its last label by the default rule `*`.
- The embedded list is version 2026-11-14_09-32-49_UTC, commit c3a53eed2cc49353c5fb5c30536186795d5c451b of github.com/publicsuffix/list. `compiler/tablegen publicsuffix public_suffix_list.dat std/net/publicsuffix` regenerates it from a downloaded `public_suffix_list.dat`. The fixtures replay the list's `tests/test_psl.txt` and check every rule of the list against the tables.
- A label that is ASCII and does not begin with `xn--` is matched as its lowercase form without UTS #46 processing, so `_dmarc` is accepted. Any other label is processed with `check_hyphens`, `use_std3_ascii_rules` and `verify_dns_length` off, and an empty label or a rejected label gives `none`.
- `http.parse_url` converts a host with non-ASCII bytes with `to_ascii`, with `check_hyphens`, `use_std3_ascii_rules` and `verify_dns_length` off, as the WHATWG URL standard does, and fails with `Invalid` when the conversion fails. `Url.host` is therefore ASCII for the `host` header, the TLS server name, the resolver, `net.dial` and the proxies. A `Domain` attribute is converted the same way, and one that is a public suffix is accepted only when it equals the request host, which makes the cookie host-only (RFC 6265bis section 5.7). A request to an IP literal skips the check.
- The tables are linked into a program only when it calls a function that reads them. A program that serves HTTP without the client links none; a program that uses the client links the mapping tables of `std/net/idna`, the tables of `std/net/publicsuffix` and the normalisation tables of `std/unicode`.
#### Locales
- `std/locale` and its sub-modules read CLDR 48.0 (the `common` directory of `cldr-common-48.0.zip`) and, for collation, `common/uca/allkeys_CLDR.txt`, `common/uca/FractionalUCA.txt` and the Unicode 17.0.0 `UnicodeData.txt`. The tables are generated: `compiler/tablegen cldr <cldr common> std/locale/data` writes the locale blobs, the supplemental tables, the plural tables and `layout.low`, and `compiler/tablegen collate <cldr common> <ucd directory> std/locale/data` writes the collation tables. A different CLDR release is a regeneration of both over that release.
- `std/locale/data/bin` holds one binary blob per CLDR locale file, 621 in all, and `locales.txt` lists the 1122 identifiers that resolve to data. A blob is a list of numbered groups (`layout.low` names them): decimal and percent patterns and symbols, compact patterns, currency names, month, weekday, quarter, day-period and era names, date and time patterns, available formats, interval formats, zone formats, relative-time patterns and list patterns. A formatter walks `Locale.blobs` from the most specific blob to the root and takes the first one that has the entry, so inheritance follows the CLDR parent chain.
- A program pays for the tables it reaches. `locale.parse` reaches every blob through `data.blob_by_id`; `locale.parse_with` with `data.chain_<id>()` reaches the blobs of one chain. Sizes of the linked program on Linux amd64: `print("hello")` is 40,952 bytes. A program that calls only the English forms of `std/text/number`, `std/time/humanize`, `std/time/datetime` and `std/time/pattern` is 117,808 bytes, 4,760 bytes more than the same program with the locale-taking functions deleted, and links no locale table. For one locale (`de`, through `parse_with`): `number.grouped_in` is 491,208 bytes, `datefmt.format` 426,120 bytes, `collate.root` 1,224,136 bytes and `collate.for_locale` for `sv` 1,638,808 bytes. For all locales (`locale.parse`): `number.grouped_in` is 4,263,816 bytes, and a program that uses `datefmt.format`, `numfmt.format_int` and `collate.for_locale` is 5,723,888 bytes. The root collation table is linked only when a collator is built, and the 191 KB of tailoring rules only when `collate.for_locale` is reached.
- Plural rules come from `plurals.xml`, `ordinals.xml` and `pluralRanges.xml` as tables that `plural.cardinal`, `plural.ordinal` and `plural.range` evaluate. `locale_plural` checks every `@integer` and `@decimal` sample of every rule of both files, and `locale_plural_range` checks the range category of 91 locale tags against `Intl.PluralRules.selectRange`.
- Number formatting follows ECMA-402 `Intl.NumberFormat` over the CLDR patterns: grouping with the minimum grouping digits of the locale, numbering systems from `-u-nu`, rounding by fraction digits, significant digits, increment and the nine rounding modes on the exact decimal, sign display, compact notation and currency symbols, narrow symbols, codes and plural names with accounting and cash digits. Parsing localized numbers is not provided.
- Date formatting is Gregorian only: the `ca` key is read and does not change the output, and the data of other calendars is not generated. `datefmt.format` prints the standard patterns of the four lengths, or the pattern the CLDR distance rules pick from the available formats for a skeleton, with the `j` skeleton letter resolved by the hour-cycle preference of the locale and `hc` overriding it. Month, weekday, era, quarter and day-period names come from the locale. A zone field prints the localized GMT offset form (`GMT+01:00`, `GMT+1`); the zone names of CLDR are not generated. `format_range` follows the interval formats of the locale.
- `std/time/pattern` accepts `%c`, `%x` and `%X` through `compile_in` and `datefmt.layouts`, which turn the standard date, time and date-time patterns of a locale into strftime layouts with ASCII digits. A standard pattern with a field no strftime verb prints, as in `la` and `zh-Hant`, fails with `NoLayout`.
- Collation implements UTS #10 over the CLDR root order (`allkeys_CLDR.txt`): 32-bit weights with implicit weights for unassigned and Han code points, contractions, expansions and canonical reordering, non-ignorable and shifted variable weighting, strengths primary to identical, backwards secondary weights, `caseFirst` upper and lower, numeric ordering of ASCII digit runs, `sort_key` and `compare`. A tailored collator compiles the CLDR rules of the locale when it is built: `&` resets (with `[before n]` and `[last regular]`), `<` through `<<<<`, `=`, expansions, `[reorder]`, `[suppressContractions]` and the `caseFirst` and `alternate` settings of the collation. The collations CLDR 48 defines without an `alt` attribute are generated for 113 locale files, selected by the locale's own tag or its `-u-co-` type (`phonebook`, `traditional`, `search`, `eor` and the rest). Absent: `zh` and `zh-Hant`, which use the root order (the rules of `zh.xml` are not generated), collations with an `alt` attribute, and the rules that use a prefix context (`|`), a logical position other than `[last regular]` (`[first ...]` and the other `[last ...]`), or `[import]`. `caseLevel`, `optimize`, a `maxVariable` other than punctuation, and numeric ordering of non-ASCII digits are not implemented. `caseFirst` is decided per collation element, with no separate class for mixed-case contractions.
- Verification. `locale_collate` runs `CollationTest_CLDR_NON_IGNORABLE_SHORT.txt` and `CollationTest_CLDR_SHIFTED_SHORT.txt` (CLDR 48 UCA conformance files) through the root collator and compares each line's sort key and the order of adjacent lines. The full files (not embedded, 18 MB and 22 MB) pass at 206,298 of 206,298 and 227,809 of 227,809 sort keys and 206,297 of 206,297 and 227,808 of 227,808 adjacent orders. The other tests compare the module with Node 24.19.0 `Intl` (ICU 78.3, CLDR 48.0, Unicode 17.0) over matrices generated by scripts kept outside the repository and embedded as TSV files: `locale_number` (10,509 rows, 86 tags: decimal, percent, scientific, compact, currency, accounting, sign display, grouping, rounding), `locale_numbers` (3,791 rows, 48 tags: `grouped_in`, `percent_in`, `compact_in`, `currency_in`), `locale_date` (6,846 rows, 81 tags), `locale_date_range` (8,836 rows, 30 tags), `locale_list` (8,640 rows, 80 tags), `locale_reltime` (7,218 rows, 81 tags), `locale_humanize` (5,400 rows, 40 tags), `locale_pattern` (765 rows, 51 tags for `%c`, `%x`, `%X`) and `locale_collate_intl` (39,217 rows, 66 tags including `-u-co-phonebk` and `-u-co-trad`, and the strength, alternate, case-first, numeric and backwards-secondary options). Every embedded row passes.
- Rows left out of the matrices because the module and `Intl` differ: 1 `fr` compact-long row (it needs the exact-count plural key `count="1"` that `numfmt` does not read); 79 `ko` rows containing the day-period names `오전` and `오후` and 18 `zh-Hant` rows with a date and a time, in `locale_date`; 44 interval rows (`bg` and `vi` `jm` across midnight, `ja` `h12` across midnight) in `locale_date_range`; and 32 collation rows, those of `fr-CA` with `ð` or `đ` and those of `da` with `caseFirst: lower` and `Aa`. Interval formats are matrix-checked for date-only skeletons and for time-only skeletons within one day; skeletons that combine a date and a time across different dates are covered by the interval rules and not by a matrix.
- Not provided: other calendars, time zone display names, CLDR units and unit formatting, `humanize.in_words` and `humanize.compact` for other languages, ordinal suffixes by locale, number parsing, and the collation features listed above.

#### HTML

- `std/html` tokenizes UTF-8 HTML following the WHATWG tokenization states and never fails. Tag and attribute names are lowercased in ASCII, attribute values may be double-quoted, single-quoted or unquoted, a repeated attribute keeps its first value, and a tag cut off by the end of input is dropped.
- A start tag for `script`, `style`, `xmp`, `iframe`, `noembed` or `noframes` switches to raw text, and one for `title` or `textarea` to escapable raw text, until the matching end tag in any case. `plaintext` takes the rest of the input. Script data follows the escaped and double-escaped states of the standard, so `<script><!--<script></script>--></script>` is one element. `noscript` is tokenized as when scripting is disabled.
- Comments end at `-->` or `--!>`, with `<!-->` and `<!--->` empty. `<?target data?>` is an `Instruction` token when the target starts with a letter or `_` and holds letters, digits, `-` and `_`; `<?xml ...>`, `<?xml-stylesheet ...>`, any other `<?...>`, `<!...>` other than comments and doctypes, `<![CDATA[...]]>` and `</` followed by a non-letter become comments, and `</>` is skipped. `Doctype` carries the lowercased name, the optional public and system identifiers and `force_quirks`.
- Character references are decoded in text, escapable raw text and attribute values. Named references match the longest name against the full WHATWG table of 2,231 names, including the names valid without a semicolon. `compiler/tablegen entities` generates the table and also writes `entity_layout.low` with the index entry layout, the name length limit and the table accessors. Decimal and hexadecimal references work with or without a semicolon. Zero, surrogates and values above U+10FFFF become U+FFFD, and 0x80 to 0x9F map through Windows-1252. Inside an attribute, a match without a semicolon that is followed by `=` or a letter or digit stays literal. Text without references is a view into the input.
- `parse` runs the tree construction algorithm of the standard over the tokenizer, which the builder drives: every insertion mode, the stack of open elements, the list of active formatting elements with the adoption agency algorithm, foster parenting for table content, `template` contents, `frameset`, the HTML integration points and the adjusted tag and attribute names of SVG and MathML, and the quirks mode decision from the doctype. `parse_fragment(context, input)` applies the fragment case with a context element, and `parse_body(input)` is the fragment case with `body`. Scripting is disabled: `noscript` content is parsed as elements. The result `Parsed` holds `root` (the document, or a fragment node), `quirks` and `errors`, the parse errors of the tree builder in the order found; the tokenizer's recoveries are not listed. The `Document` node carries the quirks mode.
- A `select` element that is not `multiple` and has a display size of 1 gets its first `selectedcontent` descendant filled with a deep copy of the children of its selected option, the last option with `selected` or else the first option that is not disabled, once parsing ends.
- `parse`, `parse_fragment`, `parse_body`, `sanitize` and `clean` take time and memory linear in the input for every input, so nesting 100,000 levels deep takes milliseconds. The stack of open elements keeps, for every entry, pointers to the nearest entry at or below it that is a scope boundary of each kind (default, list item, button, table), a special element, a list-item scan stop, a reset-insertion-mode stop and an HTML-namespace element, and a chain per element name, so "has an element in scope", the `li`, `dd` and `dt` scans, "any other end tag", foster parenting and the reset of the insertion mode do not walk the stack. The list of active formatting elements is a linked list with a marker segment per `template` or applet-like boundary and a queue of at most three entries per name and attribute set, so the Noah's Ark clause is a lookup. The adoption agency algorithm keeps the standard's bounds of 8 outer and 3 inner iterations and uses an entry pointer in place of the bookmark index. Text is appended to a byte buffer that is written to its node once, and attributes are deduplicated through a map. The one superlinear case in the standard is the reconstruction of formatting elements, which can create a new element for every formatting entry at every `p` start tag: a parse creates at most four times the input length in reconstructed elements, and after that the entries are dropped instead of recreated.
- `ParseOptions.max_depth` bounds the element nesting the way Blink and WebKit do, 512 by default (`default_max_depth`) and no limit when 0 or less. While more elements than that are open, a new element, comment or instruction becomes a child of the parent of the current node, so every node past the limit is a child of the element at the limit, and the stack of open elements keeps growing so end tags still match their start tags. Text, nodes foster parented out of a table, `template` content and nodes the adoption agency algorithm moves are placed as the standard says; a text node can sit one level below the limit, and misnested formatting markup can still build a tree deeper than the limit, as it does in browsers, so a program that walks a parsed tree must not recurse once per level. `sanitize` always parses with the default limit and parses its own output again, so its result is never deeper than the limit allows. `parse_with` and `parse_fragment_with` take the options, and `max_depth: 0` gives the standard's tree. `testdata/run/std_html_depth` holds the element count and depth of twelve nesting shapes, and the default tree of each equals Chromium's.
- A `Node` has a `data` of `Document`, `Fragment`, `Doctype`, `Element`, `Text`, `Comment` or `Instruction`, and first, last, previous and next links, so insertion and removal take constant time. An `Element` has a `Namespace` (`Html`, `Svg` or `MathMl`), a name, its attributes in source order (each with an optional namespace, prefix, name and value) and, for `template`, a content fragment. `create_element`, `create_text`, `create_comment`, `create_doctype`, `create_instruction`, `create_document` and `create_fragment` make detached nodes, `append_child` and `insert_before` fail with `HierarchyRequest` or `NotFound` and change nothing when they fail, `remove` detaches a node, `clone` copies a node or a subtree and `contains`, `children`, `parent`, `first_child`, `last_child`, `previous_sibling`, `next_sibling`, `attr`, `has_attr`, `set_attr`, `remove_attr`, `local_name`, `is_html`, `template_contents` and `text_content` read and edit it. Walks, `clone`, serialization, selector matching and `clean` are iterative, so a tree 300,000 levels deep does not exhaust the stack.
- `inner_html` and `outer_html` serialize as section 13.3 of the standard: text and attribute values escape `&` and U+00A0, attributes also `"`, text also `<` and `>`, void elements of the HTML namespace have no end tag, the children of `style`, `script`, `xmp`, `iframe`, `noembed`, `noframes` and `plaintext` are written raw, and the content of a `template` is written inside it. A `pre`, `textarea` or `listing` element does not get an added line feed, so a leading line feed in its content is lost when the text is parsed again; `reparsable_html` writes one more line feed after those start tags when the content begins with one, and parsing its result gives the same tree.
- `compile(source)` turns a selector list into a `Selector` once and `matches`, `closest`, `query` and `query_all` use it many times. It supports type and universal selectors, `.class`, `#id`, attribute selectors with `=`, `~=`, `|=`, `^=`, `$=`, `*=` and the `i` and `s` flags, the descendant, `>`, `+` and `~` combinators, selector lists, `:not()`, `:is()`, `:where()`, `:has()` with a relative selector list, `:first-child`, `:last-child`, `:only-child`, `:nth-child()` and `:nth-last-child()` with an optional `of` selector list, the `-of-type` forms, `:empty`, `:root`, `:checked`, `:disabled` and `:enabled`. A source it cannot compile fails with `SelectorError.Invalid(at, message)`, where `at` is a byte offset. Namespace prefixes, pseudo-elements, other pseudo-classes (`:hover`, `:focus`, `:lang()`, `:dir()`, `:scope`, `:required` and the rest), `||`, `&`, a `:has()` inside `:has()`, unterminated constructs, more than 1000 compound selectors in one complex selector and more than 32 nested functional pseudo-classes are errors. `:is()` and `:where()` drop an empty or invalid argument, while `:not()`, `:has()` and `nth-child` with `of` reject one.
- Matching runs right to left with backtracking, and `:has()` tests candidates below or after the subject. Type and attribute names match without regard to case on an HTML element and exactly on SVG and MathML elements; attribute values match exactly except for the attributes HTML lists as case-insensitive (`type`, `lang`, `dir`, `rel`, `method` and the like), and the `i` and `s` flags override that. `:empty` matches an element with no child element and no text, so whitespace counts as content, `:checked` reads the `checked` attribute of a checkbox or radio `input` and the `selected` attribute of an `option`, and `:root` matches the child of the document. In a document in quirks mode, `.class` and `#id` match without regard to ASCII case, and attribute selectors still compare exactly. `query` and `query_all` search the descendants of the node in document order, skip the content of `template` elements and never return the node itself; `closest` starts at the node.
- `sanitize(input, policy)` parses `input` as the content of a `body`, removes what a `Policy` does not allow and serializes the rest. A `Policy` lists the allowed HTML elements, the attributes allowed on every element and on each named element, the allowed URL schemes, whether a URL without a scheme is allowed, the elements dropped with their content, and the allowed SVG and MathML elements. An HTML element that is not listed is unwrapped, so its children take its place, unless it is in the drop list, and a foreign element that is not listed is dropped with its content. Comments, instructions and doctypes, the elements `script`, `style`, `noscript`, `template`, `iframe`, `object`, `embed`, `applet`, `noembed`, `noframes`, `plaintext`, `xmp`, `frame`, `frameset`, `base`, `link` and `meta`, the SVG elements `script`, `style`, `foreignObject`, `use`, `image`, `feImage`, `iframe` and the animation elements, and the MathML elements `annotation-xml` and `maction` are always removed. An attribute stays only when it is allowed, its name does not start with `on` and it is not `srcdoc`. The attributes that hold a URL (`href`, `src`, `srcset`, `action`, `formaction`, `poster`, `cite`, `xlink:href` and the older `background`, `data`, `longdesc`, `manifest`, `usemap`, `codebase`, `classid`, `profile`, `icon`, `lowsrc`, `dynsrc`, `imagesrcset` and `ping`) are checked as a browser reads them: tabs and line breaks anywhere and control characters and spaces at either end are ignored, a scheme is what precedes a `:` that comes before any `/`, `?` or `#`, and every candidate of a `srcset` and every URL of a `ping` must pass.
- `sanitize` serializes with `reparsable_html`. The result is checked before it is returned: parsing it and serializing the tree gives the same text and cleaning that tree removes nothing. When the check fails the output is cleaned again, and when five rounds do not settle it the result is the empty string, so no input yields markup that a second parse reads differently. `default_policy` allows headings, paragraphs, lists, tables, quotations, inline text elements, links and images with `class`, `dir`, `lang` and `title`, the `http`, `https` and `mailto` schemes and relative URLs, and no SVG or MathML. `clean(root, policy)` does the same to a tree in place.
- `testdata/run/std_html_tree` runs the html5lib tree-construction files of the web-platform-tests copy of the html5lib suite and passes every case except the ones that need scripting enabled. `testdata/run/std_html_serialize` compares `inner_html` and `outer_html` with parse5, and `testdata/run/std_html_select` compares the selector results and rejections with jsdom and soupsieve. `testdata/run/std_html_quirks` holds the mode of 41 doctypes, equal to parse5's, and the class and id counts with and without a doctype, equal to jsdom's. `testdata/run/std_html_adversarial` parses, cleans and sanitizes 25 hostile shapes (nested `div`, `b`, `a`, `table`, `svg`, `math`, `template`, `li`, `dd`, `option` and `button`, stray end tags, repeated and distinct attributes, one-character text tokens and character references) at sizes where a quadratic parser takes seconds, and its counts for the unlimited tree equal parse5's. `testdata/run/std_html_edit` compares the tree editing results and errors with jsdom, and `clean` with hand-derived counts. `testdata/run/std_html_sanitize` compares `sanitize` with a reference sanitizer written over parse5 for URL obfuscations, mutation-XSS payloads and generated markup, checks that the output holds no `script` element, event handler attribute or `javascript:` URL, and checks that it is a fixed point of parse and serialize.

#### Markdown

- `std/markdown` parses blocks with the CommonMark line algorithm: a stack of open containers, lazy continuation lines, and a list that is loose when a blank line separates its items or two of its blocks. GFM adds tables, strikethrough, task list items and extended autolinks (`http://`, `https://`, `ftp://`, `www.` and bare email addresses, with `marked`'s trailing punctuation rules). Tables need a delimiter row of the same width as the header, end at a blank line or a line that starts another block, and pad or cut body rows to the header width.
- Inline parsing scans delimiter runs into a chain and resolves emphasis, strong and strikethrough with the CommonMark flanking and rule-of-three tests, so the work is linear in practice. Links and images use a bracket stack. Inline content is parsed once per heading, paragraph or table cell, after every reference definition in the document is known.
- Escapes and entities stay in the tree as `Escape` and `Entity` nodes, so a renderer can tell `\*` from `*`. Numeric references decode and named references pass through to the HTML unchanged, as `marked` does.
- `Heading` and `Cell` carry the raw source text next to the parsed content. `plain_text` renders spans without emphasis, link or image markup: images give their alt text, character references are decoded, inline HTML stays as written and a break is a line ending.
- `render` writes the HTML `marked` writes with `gfm` on: `<br>` and `<hr>` without a slash, a `<tbody>` with no line ending before its first row, tight list items without `<p>`, a `language-` class from the first word of the info string, and `cleanUrl` for hrefs. A raw HTML block is written as parsed, so a block followed by a blank line is not followed by a line ending.
- Each hook of a `Renderer` receives the renderer, and `render` and `render_inline` take any renderer, so a hook can nest the rest of the output. The default hooks are public and can be called from a custom one.
- The output equals `marked` on `docs/design.md`, `editors/README.md` and the doc comments of the standard library. Paragraphs keep their leading and trailing whitespace as `marked` does, except at the end of a list. Known departures are in unusual input: an empty list item that follows a paragraph or a setext underline, a bare URL directly followed by `[`, a backtick or a backslash, which `marked` takes into the link, and a paragraph that ends in a whitespace-only line.

### D-Bus

- `std/dbus` does not use `std/serde`: its model is name-keyed structs, with no 16-bit integer, signature typing, variant, dict entry or descriptor, and derived code cannot produce the tuple structs of D-Bus. Values are the dynamic `Value` enum (`Byte`, `Boolean`, `Int16`, `UInt16`, `Int32`, `UInt32`, `Int64`, `UInt64`, `Double`, `String`, `ObjectPath`, `Signature`, `UnixFd`, `Bytes` for `ay`, `Array`, `Struct`, `Variant` and `DictEntry`). `Int16` carries an `i64` in -32768..32767 because Low has no 16-bit integer, and `Array` holds the element signature so an empty array keeps its type. `variant`, `strings`, `dict` and `dict_of` build values and `bool_of`, `int_of`, `uint_of`, `double_of`, `string_of`, `fd_of`, `bytes_of`, `array_of`, `fields_of` and `unwrap` read them, failing with `Marshal` on a different variant.
- `Type` is a parsed signature. `parse_signature` accepts at most 255 bytes, array nesting of 32, struct nesting of 32 and 64 in all, and rejects empty structs, a dict entry outside an array and a non-basic dict key. `parse_type` takes one complete type and `parse_element` one array element type. `valid_object_path`, `valid_interface`, `valid_member`, `valid_error_name` and `valid_bus_name` follow the specification's name rules, including the 255-byte limit and the unique-name form.
- `encode(value, order)` returns an `Encoded` (bytes and descriptor numbers) and `decode` reads either byte order. Alignment is relative to the start of the message, array lengths exclude their padding, strings and signatures must be UTF-8 without NUL, booleans are 0 or 1, padding bytes are zero, and an array over 64 MiB fails with `TooLarge`. A variant nests at most 64 deep.
- `Message` carries the type, the flags (`no_reply_expected`, `no_auto_start`, `allow_interactive_authorization`), the serial, the header fields and the body. `encode_message` writes the header `yyyyuua(yv)` padded to 8 bytes and checks the fields each type requires; `message_length` reads the fixed header from 16 bytes and returns the total length, failing with `TooLarge` over 128 MiB; `message_fd_count` returns the `UNIX_FDS` field; `decode_message` takes the message with the descriptors that accompanied it. `method_call`, `signal`, `method_return` and `error_reply` build messages, and `remote_error` turns an error message into `Remote(name, message)`.
- `parse_addresses` splits an address on `;` into `Address` values (a transport and a key map), `address_guid` reads the `guid` key, `address_text` writes an address back, `escape_address_value` and `unescape_address_value` apply the `%xx` escaping, `socket_path` resolves `unix:path=`, `unix:abstract=` and `unix:runtime=yes` (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/bus`), and `session_addresses` and `system_addresses` read `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` and `DBUS_SYSTEM_BUS_ADDRESS`, falling back to `unix:path=$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/bus` and `unix:path=/run/dbus/system_bus_socket`.
- Authentication is the SASL exchange of the specification. A client sends the NUL byte, `AUTH EXTERNAL` with the hexadecimal uid, falls back to `ANONYMOUS` after a `REJECTED`, sends `NEGOTIATE_UNIX_FD` and `BEGIN`. `ServerAuth` configures the other side (`guid`, `uid`, `anonymous`, `unix_fds`): EXTERNAL is accepted when the uid the client states equals `uid` (the effective uid of the process by default) and the peer credentials of the socket report the same uid. Lines are at most 16384 bytes.
- `connect_session`, `connect_system` and `connect_addresses` open a `Connection`; `connect_over` takes an open `net.ConnHandle` and says whether to send `Hello`; `accept_over` is the server side. `Connection` is the owner of a `ConnectionHandle`, which shares one reader green thread that matches replies to calls by serial, offers every other message to the filters added with `add_filter`, and ends when the socket closes. `call`, `call_with` (a `CallOptions` with a timeout and a `cancel.Token`) and `send` take serial numbers from the connection; pending calls fail with `Closed` when the connection ends and with `Timeout` or `Cancelled` when their option fires. A method return carries the descriptors that came with its message, and descriptors travel with a sent message through `SCM_RIGHTS` on one write.
- `request_name`, `release_name` and `name_has_owner` use the bus daemon; `add_match`, `remove_match` and `subscribe` register a `MatchRule` (`rule_text` writes it and `rule_matches` evaluates it locally), a `Subscription` delivering the matching messages on a channel. `get_property`, `set_property`, `get_all`, `ping`, `machine_id`, `introspect_xml` and `introspect` call the standard interfaces, `introspect` parsing the XML with `std/xml` into a `NodeInfo`.
- `serve` makes a `Service`, a filter that answers every method call that arrives, with `UnknownObject` for a path nothing is exported at. `export` takes an `InterfaceSpec` (methods, properties and signals); calls run each on their own green thread. `Introspectable`, `Properties` and `Peer` are answered from the specifications. A handler fails with `Remote(name, message)` to reply with that error; other errors reply `org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.Failed` or `InvalidArgs`; an unknown path, interface, method or property replies with the matching standard error. `emit_changed` sends `PropertiesChanged`.
- Every operation has a form that takes the `*ConnectionHandle` ending in `_on`, for threads the connection has been moved away from, as `### Resource ownership` describes.
- Marshalling vectors come from `jeepney` (cross-checked against GLib `GVariant`), signatures and addresses from libdbus, names from GLib. The protocol fixtures run with no daemon, over a socket pair. `std/dbus/daemon_test_linux.low` starts a private `dbus-daemon` and runs a Low service against `busctl`, `gdbus` and `dbus-send`, and a Low client against a Gio service; each test skips when a tool is missing.

### Desktop portals

- `std/portal` talks to `org.freedesktop.portal.Desktop` on the session bus. `connect()` returns a `Portal` that owns its connection and `over(handle)` one that borrows a `*dbus.ConnectionHandle`; `connect_within(ms)` limits the calls that do not wait on the user, 25 seconds otherwise. A `Portal` is bound with `using` and several green threads may use it at once.
- A call that waits on the user follows the portal's request pattern in `request(p, ask, token, stop)`: the request object path `/org/freedesktop/portal/desktop/request/<sender>/<token>` is computed from the connection's unique name (leading colon dropped, dots as underscores) and the handle token, the `Response` signal on it is subscribed to before the call, the call returns the handle, a handle other than the computed one moves the subscription, and the call waits for `Response` (a code and an `a{sv}` of results), for the connection to close, or for `stop`. Cancelling `stop` sends `Request.Close` and fails with `Cancelled`. An empty handle token takes `low1`, `low2` and on, per `Portal`.
- `open_file(p, dialog)` and `save_file(p, dialog)` take a `FileDialog` (parent window string, title, accept label, `modal`, `multiple`, `directory`, `filters`, `current_filter`, `current_name`, `current_folder`, `current_file`, token, cancel token) and return the chosen `file://` URIs and the selected filter, or `none` when the user closed the dialog; response code 2 fails with `Remote("org.freedesktop.portal.Error.Failed", ...)`. A `FileFilter` has a name, glob patterns and MIME types. `parent_window` is the portal's string, `x11:<id in hex>` or `wayland:<exported handle>`, and empty for none; `std/ui` windows do not export a Wayland handle, so they pass it empty.
- `open_uri(p, uri, request)` returns `false` when the user closed the application chooser. `read_setting`, `color_scheme` (`NoPreference`, `Dark`, `Light`), `accent_color` (`none` when a channel is outside 0 to 1) and `animations_enabled` read `org.freedesktop.portal.Settings.Read` and unwrap its variants; `watch_settings(p, namespace)` and `setting_change` follow `SettingChanged`. `add_notification`, `remove_notification`, `watch_actions` and `action_invoked` cover the notification portal with a title, body, themed icon name, priority, default action and buttons.
- Each call has a `..._call` function that returns its `dbus.Message` without sending it. `std/portal/portal_test.low` compares the marshalled bodies of those calls with `testdata/portal/*.hex`, which `testdata/portal/generate.py` writes with `jeepney`. `portal_test_linux.low` runs a Low `FileChooser` service on a private `dbus-daemon` to test the request pattern, dismissal, failure and cancellation without a dialog, and reads the colour scheme from the session's portal when one runs; each skips when its tool or portal is missing.

### Regular expressions

- `std/regex` compiles a pattern once into a program for a Pike VM, a Thompson NFA simulation that carries capture slots per thread, so matching takes time linear in the input times the program size and never backtracks.
- A `Regex` keeps two pools of idle machines: one without capture slots for `is_match`, one with them for `find`, `full_match` and the scans. Each machine owns its thread queues, capture slots and scratch. A call takes an idle machine under a lock, builds one when none is idle, and puts it back when it finishes. A compiled value is therefore shared freely between green threads, and a callback that matches with the same `Regex` during `replace_all` gets its own machine. A `Match` copies its slots out of the machine.
- Syntax: literals; escapes `\n \t \r \f \v \a`, `\xhh`, `\uhhhh`, `\Uhhhhhhhh` and escaped punctuation; `.`; classes with ranges, negation and `\d \w \s \D \W \S` inside; `\d \w \s \D \W \S \b \B \A \Z \z`; `^` and `$`; capturing groups, `(?:...)`, inline flags `(?i)`, `(?m)`, `(?s)`, `(?-i)` and scoped `(?i:...)`; alternation; `*`, `+`, `?`, `{m}`, `{m,}`, `{,n}` and `{m,n}` with lazy forms. A `{` that does not form a valid count is a literal. Inline flags apply to the rest of the enclosing group.
- Input is UTF-8 matched by code point; offsets are bytes. `\d` and `\s` are ASCII (`[0-9]` and `[\t-\r ]`).
  - `\w` is the UTS #18 word set: Alphabetic, Mark (`Mn`, `Mc`, `Me`), Decimal_Number (`Nd`), Connector_Punctuation (`Pc`) and Join_Control (U+200C and U+200D), and `\W` is its complement. `\b` and `\B` compare the word-ness of the code point before the position with that of the code point at it, so a boundary is a change in word-ness; a byte that is not valid UTF-8 is not a word character.
  - `(?i)` folds by Unicode simple case folding (`CaseFolding.txt` status C and S). A literal, a class member or every code point of a range matches all members of its orbit, so `k` matches `K` and U+212A KELVIN SIGN, `s` matches U+017F, `σ` matches `ς` and `Σ`, `ß` matches U+1E9E and `ǆ` matches `ǅ` and `Ǆ`; `(?i)[a-z]` also matches U+017F and U+212A. A class folds before it is negated, so `(?i)[^k]` matches none of the three. `\d`, `\s`, `\w` and their negations are not folded.
  - `.` excludes only `\n` unless `dot_all` is set, so it matches `\r`; `[^\r\n]` is a character outside a line terminator. Without `multiline`, `^` matches only at the start of the input and `$` at the end or before a final `\n`. With it, a line ends at `\n`, at a `\r` not followed by `\n`, or at `\r\n`: `^` matches at the start, after `\n` and after a `\r` not followed by `\n`, and `$` matches at the end, before `\r` and before a `\n` not preceded by `\r`, so neither matches between the `\r` and the `\n` of one terminator.
  - The word set and the fold orbits are `word_ranges.bin` and `fold_orbits.bin` in `std/regex`, written by `compiler/tablegen classes <ucd directory> std/regex` from `UnicodeData.txt`, `DerivedCoreProperties.txt`, `PropList.txt` and `CaseFolding.txt` of Unicode 16.0.0, the version of the category data in `std/unicode`, together with `class_layout.low`, which holds the entry sizes and the `embed` accessors. `word_ranges.bin` is 796 sorted `lo`, `hi` pairs of little-endian `u32` (6,368 bytes). `fold_orbits.bin` is 2,938 `code`, `next` pairs sorted by `code`, where `next` is the next larger member of the orbit and the largest member points to the smallest (23,504 bytes). Folding a range binary-searches the first orbit member inside it and follows each orbit, so a range as wide as the code space costs one pass over the table. `compiler/regex` embeds the same two files, so a generated pattern and `compile` build identical classes.
  - Bytes below U+0080 never reach the tables. `in_class` scans the ranges that start below U+0080 before it binary-searches the rest, and `word_before` and `word_at` test an ASCII byte directly; a non-ASCII word test decodes the code point, stepping back at most three continuation bytes for the one before the position, and binary-searches `word_ranges.bin`.
- Matching is leftmost-first, as in Python and RE2, and results for ASCII patterns and ASCII text without `\r` agree with Python's `re` under `re.ASCII`. `find_all`, `replace_all` and `split` follow Python 3.7: an empty match is allowed right after a non-empty one, and after an empty match the next one may start at the same offset only if it is not empty. `split` does not include group text. `split_n` splits at most `max` times; a negative `max` splits everywhere.
- Repeat counts are limited to 10000 and programs to 100,000 instructions. Invalid patterns fail with `Invalid(at, message)`, at the byte offset Python reports. Backreferences, lookaround, named groups and possessive quantifiers are rejected.
- `regex.generated("pattern")` is a `Regex` built at compile time, like .NET's `[GeneratedRegex]`. The pattern must be a string literal; flags are written inline. An invalid pattern is a compile error at the literal, `invalid regex at byte N: message`, with the offset and message `compile` would fail with. The result is used through the same functions as a compiled `Regex` and matches identically.
- `compiler/regex` holds the parser and assembler the checker runs on a literal pattern, held to the `std/regex` one by running the `std_regex_errors` fixture's patterns through it. The checker turns each distinct literal into Low functions named `__regex_` and the FNV-1a hash of the pattern, parsed with the prelude's privileges and added to `std/regex`, so they reach its private types.
- A `Regex` carries an `Engine` of thread-start, step and skip functions with the queue size. `compile` fills it with the Pike VM. The generated engine keeps the VM's queues, priorities and capture slots but replaces interpretation with code per instruction:
  - One function per instruction that starts a thread, follows a consumed character or joins two epsilon paths, each guarded by the queue's sparse set. The remaining jumps, splits, saves and assertions are inlined into it.
  - Characters and classes of up to four ranges compare inline in the step, which dispatches on the program counter by binary search. Larger classes get a function that tests the ranges below U+0080 first and binary-searches the rest. `^` and `$` under `multiline` call `line_start` and `line_end`, and `\b` and `\B` call `word_before` and `word_at`, the functions the interpreter uses.
  - When no empty match is possible from the start, a skip function moves past ASCII bytes that cannot begin a match before a thread starts.
  - Programs over 2000 instructions are compiled at run time by `compile` instead, so call depth stays bounded.

### Compression

- `std/compress/codec` holds what the codecs share. `Buffer` is a growable byte buffer with an LSB bit packer, and every write to it is infallible. Each encoder is a core that writes into a `Buffer`; the shared `Encoder` runs the core's `feed`, `sync` and `close` steps, drains the buffer to its sink and rejects every call after `finish` with `Invalid`, so one-shot `compress` has no error to handle. `BitReader` reads LSB-first bits from a slice or an `io.Reader` and fails with `Malformed` on short input. `Tables` builds two-level lookup tables for canonical Huffman codes, and `Builder` computes length-limited code lengths. `Chain` is a hash-chain match finder and `History` the sliding window that zstd and LZ4 index with it, `decompress` runs the one-shot decode of a codec under an output limit, `copy_match` copies an LZ77 match, and `read_varint` and `write_varint` code LEB128 integers, which `std/protobuf` and xz also use. Deflate, brotli, WebP lossless, zstd, LZ4, snappy, xz and bzip2 build on it where their formats agree.
- DEFLATE, zlib and gzip decode stored, fixed and dynamic blocks with two-level lookup tables, check Adler-32, CRC-32, ISIZE and the gzip header CRC, and read concatenated gzip members. The encoder uses greedy matching at levels 1 to 3 and lazy matching at 4 to 9 over a hash chain on a 32 KB window, choosing dynamic, fixed or stored encoding per block. `flush` emits a sync flush so streamed output is decodable as it arrives.
- xz decodes multiple blocks and streams, the none, CRC32, CRC64 and SHA-256 checks, LZMA2 chunks with their resets, and the delta filter; other filters fail with `Unsupported`. The encoder writes LZMA2 with a hash-chain match finder and lazy parsing, a dictionary from 256 KiB to 64 MiB by level, and raw chunks where compression does not pay.
- `std/compress/zstd` and `std/compress/lz4` each offer `compress(data, level)`, `decompress(data, limit)`, a streaming `decode(reader, sink, limit)` that passes output to the sink one block at a time, and a streaming `Encoder` with `encode`, `flush` and `finish`.
  - The zstd decoder handles every block and literal type, 1- and 4-stream Huffman literals, all four FSE table modes, repeat offsets, skippable and concatenated frames, and the content checksum. Its history grows with real output up to the smaller of the window and the remaining limit, so large windows never allocate what the header claims up front.
  - The zstd encoder finds matches with two hash chains keyed on 8-byte and 4- or 5-byte prefixes, with depth and lazy evaluation set by the level. It Huffman-codes literals, picks predefined, RLE or custom FSE tables per block by estimated cost, and falls back to raw or RLE blocks.
  - The LZ4 decoder accepts linked and independent blocks, block and content checksums, content sizes and skippable frames. The encoder writes linked 64 KB blocks with a content checksum.
  - Dictionaries and LZ4 frames with magic number `0x184C2102` fail with `Unsupported`.
- `std/compress/brotli` has the same API as zstd and LZ4.
  - The decoder handles compressed, uncompressed and metadata meta-blocks, block switching for literals, commands and distances, all four literal context modes, context maps with run-length coding and inverse move-to-front, distance postfix and direct parameters, the static dictionary with all 121 transforms, and windows of 10 to 24 bits. Output collects in a ring buffer that starts at 64 KB and doubles up to the window, and reaches the sink at the end of each meta-block, when the ring wraps, and before each blocking read. The 122,784-byte dictionary is `embed`ded from `dictionary.bin`.
  - The encoder writes uncompressed meta-blocks at level 0. Levels 1 to 11 run hash chains of depth 2^(level-1) with lazy matching from level 4 and checks against the four recent distances and their neighbours, writing 256 KB meta-blocks with Huffman-coded commands and distances. From level 5 they model literals in UTF-8 context mode with up to 3·level-11 clustered trees. A block that would grow is stored. `flush` ends the meta-block and pads with an empty metadata block so everything sent decodes.
- `std/bits` holds a growable `Set` over `u64[]` words. Counts use a SWAR popcount, and scans (`next_set`, `next_clear`, `min`, `max`, `rank`, `select_at`, `members`) step a word at a time.
- `std/bits/roaring` holds `Roaring`, a set of `u32` split into 65536-value chunks keyed by the high 16 bits. Each chunk is a sorted `u16[]` array of up to 4096 values, a 1024-word bitmap, or a list of runs. Arrays and bitmaps convert at 4096 values; ranges create runs, and unions and intersections of runs stay runs. Every container tracks its cardinality, so `cardinality` is constant time. Set operations come as new-set and in-place `_with` forms, working word by word on bitmaps and by galloping merge on arrays. `run_optimize` picks the smallest form per container by the Go library's rule. `serialize` and `deserialize` implement the portable Roaring format byte for byte as Go `roaring/v2` writes it, rejecting any inconsistent length, ordering or cardinality with `Malformed`.
- `std/image` holds an `Image` of width, height, `Format` (`Gray8`, `GrayAlpha8`, `Rgb8`, `Rgba8`, `Gray16`, `Rgba16`, 16-bit samples big-endian), stride and pixel bytes.
  - Pixels read and write as 8-bit `Rgba`, with operations converting through 16-bit intermediates. `get` outside the image is transparent black and `set` there does nothing. Operations convert formats, crop, flip, rotate, apply EXIF orientation, fill rectangles, composite straight alpha, premultiply and unpremultiply, and resize separably on premultiplied values with nearest, bilinear, Catmull-Rom bicubic or Lanczos-3 filters.
  - `decode` picks a codec by signature and rejects images over 2^26 pixels; `decode_limited` takes another limit.
  - PNG decodes every colour type, bit depth and Adam7, checks every CRC, and encodes with per-row adaptive filtering. The result is `Gray16` for 16-bit gray without transparency, `Rgba16` for other 16-bit images, `Gray8` or `GrayAlpha8` for gray, `Rgba8` for colour with alpha or `tRNS`, and `Rgb8` otherwise. `decode_png` decodes only PNG and fails with `Unsupported` on any other signature. `gAMA`, `cHRM`, `sRGB` and `iCCP` chunks are not read, so samples are returned as stored; the PngSuite in `testdata/ui_png` checks the result against ImageMagick's.
  - JPEG decodes baseline and progressive scans with restart markers, any sampling, grayscale and Adobe CMYK/YCCK, with libjpeg's integer IDCT and fancy upsampling, so output matches libjpeg byte for byte. It encodes baseline 4:2:0 with the standard tables scaled by quality.
  - GIF decodes 87a and 89a and composites every frame by disposal method in `decode_gif_frames`. It encodes one frame with a given or median-cut palette.
  - BMP covers 1 to 32 bits, RLE, bitfields and every header version, and encodes 24 or 32 bits.
  - QOI decodes and encodes; the result is `Rgb8` or `Rgba8` by channel count.
  - WebP decodes lossless VP8L with every transform, the colour cache and meta prefix codes. Lossy VP8, alpha chunks and animation fail with `Unsupported`.
- Every codec has buffer functions, a streaming decoder that pulls from an `io.Reader`, pushes output to a sink and fails with `TooLarge` past a caller's limit, and a streaming encoder.

### Web

- `web.app()` holds routes and middleware. `get`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `del` and `route` add handlers for paths with `:name` parameters and a trailing `*`. `group` makes a sub-application with a prefix and more middleware that registers its routes on the same router. An unknown path is a 404. A known path with another method runs the middleware of the first route with that path, then answers 405 with `Allow`, so `cors` answers a preflight for any route.
- `endpoint[Req, Res]` and `query_endpoint[Res]` read a JSON body into `Req`, call a handler returning `Res ! web.Problem`, and write the result as JSON or the problem as `application/problem+json`. Their schemas describe the operation in the OpenAPI document that `openapi` serves, along with path parameters and each route's security schemes.
- Middleware is a struct of a `run` closure and an optional security scheme. `api_key`, `basic` and `bearer` authenticate requests and set `Context.user`. `sessions` keeps a map in a cookie sealed with AES-256-GCM, with the cookie name as associated data and an expiry inside. `csrf` requires the session's token in `X-CSRF-Token` on unsafe methods. `require_session`, `cors` (with the request headers it allows), `logger` and `compress` complete the set; `compress` picks Zstandard, Brotli or gzip, in that order, from what `Accept-Encoding` allows.
- `jwt` verifies bearer tokens against a key set with `std/crypto/jose`. `oidc` does the same for an OpenID Connect issuer: it reads the discovery document and the JWKS, refreshes them hourly, and refreshes early, at most once a minute, when a token names a key it does not know. `oidc_login` adds a sign-in route that redirects to the issuer with `state`, `nonce` and a PKCE S256 challenge kept in the session, and a callback route that checks the state, exchanges the code, verifies the ID token and its nonce, and hands the claims to the application.
- A handler streams a body by returning a `Response` with `stream` set to a `chan[u8[]]` and closing it at the end. The server writes each chunk as it arrives, cancels `req.token` when the stream ends, the client disconnects or a write fails, and drops the stream for `HEAD` and bodiless statuses. Response compression skips streamed responses.
- `sse.stream` sets `text/event-stream`, `no-cache` and `x-accel-buffering: no`, flushes every event, sends a `: keepalive` comment every 15 seconds unless `keepalive` is 0, and stops when `req.token` is cancelled. `Emitter.send` returns false after the client has gone. `sse.format` removes CR and LF from `id` and `event` and NUL from `id`.
- `web.static` decodes each path segment and answers 400 for `..`, an embedded separator, NUL or a bad escape, and 404 for dotfiles, non-regular files and any file whose real path leaves the root. A directory without a trailing slash is redirected, and a directory serves `index.html` or an optional listing. `precompressed` picks `.br` then `.gz` by `Accept-Encoding`. A multi-range or malformed `Range` gives the whole file. Files stream in 64 KiB chunks.
## Compiler

The compiler is written in Low, in `compiler/`. Pipeline:

| Stage | Module | Output |
|---|---|---|
| Lex | `compiler/lexer` | tokens |
| Parse | `compiler/parser` | `compiler/ast` tree |
| Check | `compiler/check` | types attached to the tree |
| Build IR | `compiler/ir` | SSA form, one value per machine word |
| Codegen | `compiler/amd64` | x86-64 GNU assembly |
| Codegen | `compiler/wasm` | WebAssembly module, for `browser/wasm32` |
| Assemble | `compiler/asm` | object: sections, symbols and fixups |
| Link | `compiler/link` | ELF or PE executable |

Building a program and running `low dev` need no external tool.

- `low` builds and runs on Linux and on Windows. A target is an operating system and an architecture: `linux/amd64`, `windows/amd64` and `browser/wasm32`. The default target is the host; `--target` selects one. The native targets, the amd64 ones, are assembled and linked in process. `browser/wasm32` goes from the SSA straight to a WebAssembly module, as `### WebAssembly` describes, and `low asm` rejects it.
- On the Windows target, `std/sys` is built from its `_windows.low` files, which call kernel32, ws2_32, iphlpapi, shell32 and crypt32, so files, processes, sockets, DNS, terminals, the environment and the system root certificates work there. `std/sql/sqlserver` calls secur32 for integrated authentication. `__syscall` is a compile error on the Windows target: the checker fails at the call with `windows/amd64 has no system calls, so __syscall cannot be called`, so a standard library file without a `_windows.low` counterpart that calls it stops the build instead of panicking at run time.
  - A descriptor is a handle. Descriptors 0 to 2 name the standard handles. A socket's descriptor packs the socket in its low 32 bits and a manual-reset event in its high 32 bits. Sockets are overlapped and non-blocking. `wait_socket` issues an overlapped `IOCTL_AFD_POLL` request for the socket's read or write conditions with the descriptor's event, and parks on the event until the request completes, which is at once when the socket is already ready. `write_view` duplicates the socket with `WSADuplicateSocketW` and gives the copy its own event, so a reader and a writer of one connection wait on separate requests and events. `shutdown` cancels the socket's pending requests with `CancelIoEx`, which wakes the waiters on every descriptor of the socket. Closing the descriptor reads and discards unread input first, because Windows answers a close with unread input by a reset that discards data the peer has not yet read, then closes both.
  - Pipes made by `pipe(true)` and `pipe_for_child` are overlapped named pipes: `CreateNamedPipeW` with `FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED` and a unique `\\.\pipe\low.<pid>.<random>` name, and a `CreateFileW` client end. Their descriptor packs the handle in the low 31 bits, a tag in bit 31 and a manual-reset event in the high 32 bits, so it never collides with a socket's descriptor, whose bit 31 is clear. A read or write issues an overlapped `ReadFile` or `WriteFile` with the descriptor's event and, when the call is pending, parks on the event with `wait_readable`, then collects the count with `GetOverlappedResult`. `ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE` ends a read with `0` and fails a write with `BrokenPipe`. Closing the descriptor through a view made by `wrap` cancels the pending operations with `CancelIoEx`, which fail with `NotConnected`, and the guard then closes the handle and the event once the last operation has returned. A read or write that has entered its lease but not yet issued its call when the descriptor is closed is not woken, and the descriptor stays open until that operation completes by itself. `open_pipe` opens an existing named pipe with `CreateFileW` and `FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED`, `SECURITY_SQOS_PRESENT` and `SECURITY_IDENTIFICATION`, retries `ERROR_PIPE_BUSY` every 20 milliseconds for a second, and returns the same tagged descriptor. `pipe_for_child` makes the parent's end overlapped and the child's end synchronous, because the child's runtime calls `ReadFile` and `WriteFile` without an `OVERLAPPED`; `pipe(false)` stays an anonymous `CreatePipe` pair. A read from any other pipe or a file holds an OS thread until it returns; `wait_input` parks on a console handle until it is signalled and otherwise sleeps a millisecond between tries.
